```tsx
// src/app/screens/MapScreen.tsx
import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from 'react';
import { UI_LINES } from '../audio/uiLines';
import { Buddy, type BuddyMove } from '../buddy/Buddy';
import { suggestNext, type PlayContext } from '../game/setRunner';
import type { BuddyLook } from '../mechanics/types';
import type { Services } from '../services';
import { artUrl } from '../ui/art';
import { useLater } from '../ui/hooks';
import { IconButton } from '../ui/IconButton';
import { Picture } from '../ui/Picture';
import { ISLANDS, islandSpots } from './islands';
import './map.css';

/**
 * Spec §4.3: five islands on one scrolling map, every spot open. Buddy points at a suggested spot — never on the
 * island or topic just played (spec §5.4 rule 6) — and says so; the child may tap any spot.
 */
export function MapScreen({ services, play, look, stickers, onPlay, onStickers }: {
  services: Services;
  play: PlayContext;
  look: BuddyLook;
  stickers: number;
  onPlay: (topicId: string) => void;
  onStickers: () => void;
}) {
  const later = useLater();
  const [suggested, setSuggested] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [move, setMove] = useState<BuddyMove>('idle');
  const spots = useRef(new Map<string, HTMLElement>());

  useEffect(() => {
    let live = true;
    suggestNext(play).then((topicId) => {
      if (!live) return;
      setSuggested(topicId);
      spots.current.get(topicId)?.scrollIntoView?.({ behavior: 'smooth', inline: 'center', block: 'nearest' });
      services.speaker.say(UI_LINES.suggest);
      setMove('point');
      later(() => setMove('idle'), 1800);
    }, (e: unknown) => console.error('no suggestion', e));
    return () => {
      live = false;
    };
  }, [later, play, services]);

  return (
    <div className="screen map">
      <div className="map__sea" role="list">
        {islandSpots(services.pack).map(({ subject, topics }) => {
          const island = ISLANDS[subject];
          return (
            <section key={subject} className="island" style={{ background: island.colour }} role="listitem" aria-label={island.title}>
              <header className="island__title">
                <img src={artUrl(island.icon)} alt="" />
                <span>{island.title}</span>
              </header>
              <div className="island__spots">
                {topics.map((t) => (
                  <button
                    key={t.id}
                    ref={(el) => { if (el) spots.current.set(t.id, el); }}
                    type="button"
                    className={`spot ${suggested === t.id ? 'is-suggested' : ''}`}
                    aria-label={t.title}
                    onClick={() => onPlay(t.id)}
                  >
                    {suggested === t.id && <img className="spot__arrow" src={artUrl('ui/point.svg')} alt="" />}
                    <Picture asset={t.asset ?? island.icon} label="" size={110} />
                  </button>
                ))}
              </div>
            </section>
          );
        })}
      </div>
      <div className="map__stickers">
        <IconButton icon="ui/stickers.svg" label="Sticker book" onClick={onStickers} />
        {stickers > 0 && <span className="map__count">{stickers}</span>}
      </div>
      <div className="map__buddy"><Buddy {...look} move={move} size={170} /></div>
    </div>
  );
}
```

```tsx
// src/app/screens/Hello.tsx
import type { Profile } from '../../engine/progress/types';
import { Buddy, isAccessory, isBuddyColour, type BuddyExpression, type BuddyMove } from '../buddy/Buddy';
import type { BuddyLook } from '../mechanics/types';
import { artUrl } from '../ui/art';
import { IconButton } from '../ui/IconButton';
import './hello.css';

/** A profile's Buddy, falling back to grey and a cap if a stored value is unknown. */
export function lookOf(p: Profile): BuddyLook {
  return { colour: isBuddyColour(p.buddy.colour) ? p.buddy.colour : 'grey', accessory: isAccessory(p.buddy.accessory) ? p.buddy.accessory : 'cap' };
}

/**
 * Spec §4.1 Hello: one child, one big Buddy to tap (the tap that unlocks audio, spec §7); several, their Buddies side by
 * side, no reading needed. The small + is for a grown-up adding a child.
 */
export function Hello({ profiles, greeting, onStart, onAddChild }: {
  profiles: readonly Profile[];
  /** while Buddy greets the child who tapped */
  greeting: { profileId: string; move: BuddyMove; expression: BuddyExpression } | null;
  onStart: (profile: Profile) => void;
  onAddChild: () => void;
}) {
  const one = profiles.length === 1;
  return (
    <div className="screen hello">
      <div className="hello__buddies">
        {profiles.map((p) => {
          const greeted = greeting?.profileId === p.id;
          return (
            <button key={p.id} type="button" className="hello__buddy" aria-label={`${p.name}'s ${p.buddy.name || 'Buddy'}`} onClick={() => onStart(p)} disabled={greeting !== null}>
              <Buddy {...lookOf(p)} size={one ? Math.min(window.innerHeight * 0.62, 460) : Math.min(window.innerHeight * 0.42, 300)} move={greeted ? greeting.move : 'idle'} expression={greeted ? greeting.expression : 'neutral'} />
              <span className="hello__name">{p.name}</span>
              {one && !greeting && <img className="hello__tap" src={artUrl('ui/point.svg')} alt="" />}
            </button>
          );
        })}
      </div>
      <IconButton className="hello__add" icon="ui/add.svg" label="Add a child (grown-ups)" onClick={onAddChild} />
    </div>
  );
}
```

```tsx
// src/app/screens/Setup.tsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import { ACCESSORIES, Buddy, BUDDY_COLOURS, type Accessory, type BuddyColour } from '../buddy/Buddy';
import './setup.css';

export interface NewChild {
  name: string;
  buddy: { colour: BuddyColour; name: string; accessory: Accessory };
}

/** Names to offer for Buddy, each spoken when tapped (spec §4.7: typed by the parent or chosen from spoken suggestions). */
export const NAME_IDEAS = ['Gajju', 'Appu', 'Motu', 'Bubbles', 'Coco', 'Peanut'] as const;

/**
 * For a grown-up (spec §4.7): the child's name, then Buddy's colour, name and accessory, with Buddy shown as chosen.
 * Buddy is called "Buddy" until named; the child's name is needed (lines greet the child by it).
 */
export function Setup({ onDone, onCancel, speakText }: { onDone: (child: NewChild) => void; onCancel?: () => void; speakText: (text: string) => void }) {
  const [name, setName] = useState('');
  const [colour, setColour] = useState<BuddyColour>('grey');
  const [buddyName, setBuddyName] = useState('');
  const [accessory, setAccessory] = useState<Accessory>('cap');
  const childName = name.trim();
  return (
    <div className="screen setup">
      <form
        className="setup__form card"
        onSubmit={(e) => {
          e.preventDefault();
          if (childName) onDone({ name: childName, buddy: { colour, name: buddyName.trim(), accessory } });
        }}
      >
        <h1>Set up Buddy</h1>
        <label className="setup__field">
          <span>Child's name</span>
          <input value={name} onChange={(e) => setName(e.target.value)} autoComplete="off" maxLength={24} required />
        </label>
        <fieldset className="setup__field">
          <legend>Buddy's colour</legend>
          <div className="setup__choices">
            {(Object.keys(BUDDY_COLOURS) as BuddyColour[]).map((c) => (
              <button key={c} type="button" className={`setup__swatch ${c === colour ? 'is-on' : ''}`} style={{ background: BUDDY_COLOURS[c].fill }} aria-label={c} aria-pressed={c === colour} onClick={() => setColour(c)} />
            ))}
          </div>
        </fieldset>
        <label className="setup__field">
          <span>Buddy's name</span>
          <input value={buddyName} onChange={(e) => setBuddyName(e.target.value)} placeholder="Buddy" autoComplete="off" maxLength={20} />
        </label>
        <div className="setup__choices">
          {NAME_IDEAS.map((idea) => (
            <button key={idea} type="button" className={`setup__chip ${idea === buddyName ? 'is-on' : ''}`} onClick={() => { setBuddyName(idea); speakText(idea); }}>{idea}</button>
          ))}
        </div>
        <fieldset className="setup__field">
          <legend>Accessory</legend>
          <div className="setup__choices">
            {ACCESSORIES.map((a) => (
              <button key={a} type="button" className={`setup__chip ${a === accessory ? 'is-on' : ''}`} aria-pressed={a === accessory} onClick={() => setAccessory(a)}>{a}</button>
            ))}
          </div>
        </fieldset>
        <div className="setup__actions">
          {onCancel && <button type="button" className="setup__secondary" onClick={onCancel}>Cancel</button>}
          <button type="submit" className="setup__go" disabled={!childName}>Let's play!</button>
        </div>
        <p className="setup__tip">Tip: add Buddy to the home screen (in Safari: Share → Add to Home Screen). It then opens full screen, works offline, and the browser will not clear the child's progress.</p>
        <p className="setup__credits">
          Pictures: <a href="https://openmoji.org" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">OpenMoji</a>, the open-source emoji project, some combined or redrawn for Buddy, and drawings in its style — <a href="https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer">CC BY-SA 4.0</a>.
        </p>
      </form>
      <div className="setup__preview"><Buddy colour={colour} accessory={accessory} size={Math.min(window.innerHeight * 0.6, 420)} /></div>
    </div>
  );
}
```

```tsx
// src/app/screens/StickerBook.tsx
import { useState } from 'react';
import type { ContentPack } from '../../engine/content/load';
import type { SubjectId } from '../../engine/content/schema';
import type { Sticker } from '../../engine/progress/types';
import { pageOf } from '../game/stickers';
import { artUrl } from '../ui/art';
import { IconButton } from '../ui/IconButton';
import { Picture } from '../ui/Picture';
import { ISLANDS } from './islands';
import './stickers.css';

/** Empty places shown on every page, so a page is never a bare "nothing here". */
const PAGE_SLOTS = 12;

/** Spec §4.6: the sticker book, one page per island (Home's page arrives with Home, Plan 1C). Nothing is ever removed. */
export function StickerBook({ pack, stickers, onBack }: { pack: ContentPack; stickers: readonly Sticker[]; onBack: () => void }) {
  const pages = Object.keys(ISLANDS) as SubjectId[];
  const [page, setPage] = useState<SubjectId>(() => {
    const latest = stickers.at(-1);
    const p = latest ? pageOf(pack, latest) : 'literacy';
    return p === 'home' ? 'literacy' : p;
  });
  const onPage = stickers.filter((s) => pageOf(pack, s) === page);
  return (
    <div className="screen book">
      <header className="book__bar">
        <IconButton icon="ui/back.svg" label="Back to the map" onClick={onBack} />
        <nav className="book__tabs">
          {pages.map((p) => (
            <button key={p} type="button" className={`book__tab ${p === page ? 'is-on' : ''}`} style={{ background: ISLANDS[p].colour }} aria-label={ISLANDS[p].title} aria-pressed={p === page} onClick={() => setPage(p)}>
              <img src={artUrl(ISLANDS[p].icon)} alt="" />
            </button>
          ))}
        </nav>
      </header>
      <div className="book__page card">
        {onPage.map((s, i) => (
          <span key={s.id ?? i} className={`book__sticker ${s.shiny ? 'is-shiny' : ''}`} style={{ transform: `rotate(${((i * 7) % 9) - 4}deg)` }}>
            <Picture asset={s.pictureId} label="sticker" size={92} />
          </span>
        ))}
        {Array.from({ length: Math.max(0, PAGE_SLOTS - onPage.length) }, (_, i) => <span key={`empty${i}`} className="book__sticker is-empty" />)}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}
```

---

### Task 23: The app: from first run to the Map and back

`App` holds which screen is up: setup on first run (asking the browser to keep the data, §10), Hello, the Map, a set, the sticker book. Tapping Buddy unlocks audio inside the tap, before any await (§7), greets the child by name for the time of day, and opens the visit: a new session, a record clock starting after the last record, the dances Buddy knows, the stickers so far. If storage fails here the child still plays (§13). "Your turn" comes every second or third set, at random (§4.5). The app-level tests play every topic on the Map to the end with a child who taps anything, and check the record each set leaves. A guard test checks that every picture the app names in its own code (icons, islands, vignette parts) is in `public/art` — `npm run validate` checks only the content's, and a renamed path would show a blank stand-in with no error in jsdom.

**Files:**
- Create: `src/app/App.tsx`, `src/app/App.test.tsx`, `src/app/art.test.ts`
- Modify: `src/app/main.tsx`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests, and the picture guard**

**File: `src/app/App.test.tsx`**

```tsx
// src/app/App.test.tsx
// @vitest-environment jsdom
import 'fake-indexeddb/auto';
import { fireEvent, render, screen } from '@testing-library/react';
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
import { App, GREETING_MS, yourTurnDue } from './App';
import { DANCE_MS, PARTY_MS, STICKER_MS } from './screens/Celebration';
import { playAnyhow, settle, useAppTimers, wait } from './testing/play';
import { testServices } from './testing/services';

beforeEach(() => { useAppTimers(); });
afterEach(() => vi.useRealTimers());

async function setUpAndStart() {
  const services = testServices();
  render(<App services={services} />);
  await settle();
  fireEvent.change(screen.getByLabelText("Child's name"), { target: { value: 'Asha' } });
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByLabelText('pink'));
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByText('Gajju'));
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByText("Let's play!"));
  await settle();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByLabelText("Asha's Gajju"));
  await wait(GREETING_MS);
  return services;
}

describe('App', () => {
  it('first run: set up Buddy, say hello, and the Map opens with a suggestion', async () => {
    const services = await setUpAndStart();
    expect(services.voice.said[0]).toMatchObject({ lineId: expect.stringMatching(/^buddy\.greet_/), slots: { name: 'Asha', buddy: 'Gajju' } });
    expect(screen.getAllByRole('listitem')).toHaveLength(5);
    expect(document.querySelectorAll('.spot')).toHaveLength(10);
    expect(document.querySelector('.spot.is-suggested')).not.toBeNull();
    expect(services.voice.said.some((r) => r.lineId === 'buddy.suggest')).toBe(true);
  });

  it('plays a whole set: every round recorded, the set\'s result, a sticker, a dance, then the Map again', async () => {
    const services = await setUpAndStart();
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByLabelText('Feelings'));
    await settle();
    expect(await playAnyhow()).toBe(true);
    await wait(PARTY_MS + STICKER_MS + DANCE_MS + 1000);
    expect(document.querySelector('.map')).not.toBeNull();
    expect(document.querySelector('.map__count')?.textContent).toBe('1');
    expect(services.danced).toHaveLength(1);
    const attempts = await services.db.attempts.toArray();
    const sets = await services.db.setResults.toArray();
    expect(attempts.length).toBe(sets[0]!.total);
    expect(sets).toHaveLength(1);
    expect(sets[0]!.correct).toBe(attempts.filter((a) => a.outcome === 'correct').length);
    expect(await services.db.stickers.count()).toBe(1);
  }, 30_000);

  it('the Home button leaves a set: its rounds stay recorded, but it has no result and earns no sticker', async () => {
    const services = await setUpAndStart();
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByLabelText('Letters A–Z'));
    await settle();
    const right = () => [...document.querySelectorAll<HTMLElement>('.stage button.option')];
    for (let i = 0; i < 6 && (await services.db.attempts.count()) < 1; i++) {
      fireEvent.click(right()[0]!);
      await wait(1800);
    }
    fireEvent.click(screen.getByLabelText('Home'));
    await settle();
    expect(document.querySelector('.map')).not.toBeNull();
    expect(await services.db.attempts.count()).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(await services.db.setResults.count()).toBe(0);
    expect(await services.db.stickers.count()).toBe(0);
  }, 30_000);

  it('plays every topic on the Map to the end', async () => {
    const services = await setUpAndStart();
    const topics = [...services.pack.topics.values()];
    for (const topic of topics) {
      fireEvent.click(screen.getByLabelText(topic.title));
      await settle();
      expect(await playAnyhow(), topic.id).toBe(true);
      await wait(PARTY_MS + STICKER_MS + DANCE_MS + 20_000); // "your turn" may follow the dance
      expect(document.querySelector('.map'), topic.id).not.toBeNull();
    }
    expect(await services.db.setResults.count()).toBe(topics.length);
    expect(await services.db.stickers.count()).toBe(topics.length);
  }, 120_000);
});

describe('yourTurnDue', () => {
  it('never on the first set since the last, sometimes on the second, always by the third (spec §4.5)', () => {
    expect(yourTurnDue(1, () => 0)).toBe(false);
    expect(yourTurnDue(2, () => 0.4)).toBe(true);
    expect(yourTurnDue(2, () => 0.6)).toBe(false);
    expect(yourTurnDue(3, () => 0.99)).toBe(true);
  });
});
```

```ts
// src/app/art.test.ts
import { existsSync, readdirSync, readFileSync, statSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';

const APP = fileURLToPath(new URL('.', import.meta.url));
const ART = fileURLToPath(new URL('../../public/art/', import.meta.url));

/** The app's own source files: no tests, no test helpers. */
function sources(dir: string): string[] {
  return readdirSync(dir).flatMap((name) => {
    const path = join(dir, name);
    if (statSync(path).isDirectory()) return name === 'testing' ? [] : sources(path);
    return /\.tsx?$/.test(name) && !/\.test\.tsx?$/.test(name) ? [path] : [];
  });
}

describe('the pictures the app names in its own code', () => {
  it('are all in public/art — icons, islands, vignette parts (validate checks the content\'s)', () => {
    // a renamed path would show a blank stand-in, and jsdom never fires the image's error. Any folder, so new art is
    // covered; but `vignettes/…` are the keys of the composed vignettes (scenario/vignettes.ts), not files.
    const literal = /['"`](?!vignettes\/)([\w-]+\/[\w-]+\.svg)['"`]/g;
    const named = new Set(sources(APP).flatMap((file) => [...readFileSync(file, 'utf8').matchAll(literal)].map((m) => m[1] as string)));
    expect(named.size).toBeGreaterThan(28);
    for (const path of named) expect(existsSync(ART + path), path).toBe(true);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the app and its entry point**

**File: `src/app/App.tsx`**

```tsx
// src/app/App.tsx
import { useEffect, useRef, useState } from 'react';
import { createProfile, lastRecordedAt, listProfiles, STARTER_DANCES, startSession, stickersForProfile } from '../engine/progress/db';
import type { Profile, Session, Sticker } from '../engine/progress/types';
import { blockFor, greetingLineFor } from '../engine/time';
import type { Names } from './audio/speaker';
import type { BuddyExpression, BuddyMove } from './buddy/Buddy';
import { monotonicClock } from './game/clock';
import { within } from './game/storage';
import type { PlayContext } from './game/setRunner';
import type { BuddyLook } from './mechanics/types';
import { GameScreen } from './screens/GameScreen';
import { Hello, lookOf } from './screens/Hello';
import { MapScreen } from './screens/MapScreen';
import { Setup, type NewChild } from './screens/Setup';
import { StickerBook } from './screens/StickerBook';
import type { Services } from './services';

type Screen =
  | { name: 'boot' }
  | { name: 'setup' }
  | { name: 'hello' }
  | { name: 'map' }
  | { name: 'game'; topicId: string; yourTurn: boolean }
  | { name: 'stickers' };

/** One child's visit: who plays, and the record-keeping context for their sets. */
interface Visit {
  profile: Profile;
  look: BuddyLook;
  names: Names;
  dances: readonly string[];
  play: PlayContext;
}

/** How long Buddy's greeting has before the Map opens. */
export const GREETING_MS = 2800;

/** Spec §4.5: "your turn" every 2nd–3rd set, at random; `sets` counts the sets since the last one, this one included. */
export function yourTurnDue(sets: number, random: () => number): boolean {
  return sets >= 3 || (sets === 2 && random() < 0.5);
}

/** The app (spec §4.1, Plan 1B's part): Setup on first run, Hello, the Map, a set, the sticker book. */
export function App({ services }: { services: Services }) {
  const [screen, setScreen] = useState<Screen>({ name: 'boot' });
  const [profiles, setProfiles] = useState<Profile[]>([]);
  const [visit, setVisit] = useState<Visit | null>(null);
  const [greeting, setGreeting] = useState<{ profileId: string; move: BuddyMove; expression: BuddyExpression } | null>(null);
  const [stickers, setStickers] = useState<readonly Sticker[]>([]);
  const setsSinceYourTurn = useRef(0);

  useEffect(() => {
    listProfiles(services.db).then(
      (ps) => {
        setProfiles(ps);
        setScreen(ps.length > 0 ? { name: 'hello' } : { name: 'setup' });
      },
      (e: unknown) => {
        console.error('could not read profiles', e);
        setScreen({ name: 'setup' });
      },
    );
  }, [services]);

  const addChild = async (child: NewChild) => {
    const profile = await createProfile(services.db, child);
    // spec §10: ask the browser to keep this data (installed apps are exempt from clean-up anyway)
    void navigator.storage?.persist?.().catch(() => false);
    setProfiles((ps) => [...ps, profile]);
    setScreen({ name: 'hello' });
  };

  /** The visit's session and clock; on a storage failure the child still plays, the record kept in memory. */
  const openVisit = async (profile: Profile): Promise<Visit> => {
    const now = services.now();
    const read = async <T,>(r: () => Promise<T>, fallback: T) => within(r()).catch((e: unknown) => {
      services.storage.failures += 1;
      services.storage.lastError = e;
      return fallback;
    });
    const [session, last, unlocks, earned] = await Promise.all([
      read<Session>(() => startSession(services.db, profile.id), { profileId: profile.id, index: 1, startedAt: now }),
      read(() => lastRecordedAt(services.db, profile.id), 0),
      read(() => services.db.unlocks.get(profile.id), undefined),
      read(() => stickersForProfile(services.db, profile.id), [] as Sticker[]),
    ]);
    setStickers(earned);
    return {
      profile,
      look: lookOf(profile),
      names: { name: profile.name, buddy: profile.buddy.name },
      dances: unlocks?.dances ?? STARTER_DANCES,
      play: {
        pack: services.pack, db: services.db, profileId: profile.id, sessionIndex: session.index, sessionStartedAt: session.startedAt,
        clock: monotonicClock(last), now: services.now, writes: services.writes, storage: services.storage,
        random: services.random, newSeed: services.newSeed,
      },
    };
  };

  const start = (profile: Profile) => {
    services.unlockAudio(); // inside the tap, before any await (spec §7)
    services.speaker.names = { name: profile.name, buddy: profile.buddy.name };
    setGreeting({ profileId: profile.id, move: 'wave', expression: 'happy' });
    services.speaker.say(greetingLineFor(blockFor(new Date(services.now()))));
    void Promise.all([openVisit(profile), new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, GREETING_MS))]).then(([v]) => {
      setVisit(v);
      setGreeting(null);
      setScreen({ name: 'map' });
    });
  };

  const finished = (yourTurn: boolean) => {
    setsSinceYourTurn.current = yourTurn ? 0 : setsSinceYourTurn.current + 1;
    setScreen({ name: 'map' });
    if (visit) void services.writes.idle().then(() => stickersForProfile(services.db, visit.profile.id)).then(setStickers, () => {});
  };

  switch (screen.name) {
    case 'boot':
      return <div className="screen" />;
    case 'setup':
      return <Setup onDone={(c) => void addChild(c).catch((e: unknown) => console.error('could not save the profile', e))} {...(profiles.length > 0 ? { onCancel: () => setScreen({ name: 'hello' }) } : {})} speakText={services.speakText} />;
    case 'hello':
      return <Hello profiles={profiles} greeting={greeting} onStart={start} onAddChild={() => setScreen({ name: 'setup' })} />;
    case 'map':
      return visit && (
        <MapScreen
          services={services}
          play={visit.play}
          look={visit.look}
          stickers={stickers.length}
          onPlay={(topicId) => setScreen({ name: 'game', topicId, yourTurn: yourTurnDue(setsSinceYourTurn.current + 1, services.random) })}
          onStickers={() => setScreen({ name: 'stickers' })}
        />
      );
    case 'game':
      return visit && (
        <GameScreen
          services={services}
          play={visit.play}
          look={visit.look}
          names={visit.names}
          topicId={screen.topicId}
          dances={visit.dances}
          yourTurn={screen.yourTurn}
          onLeave={() => setScreen({ name: 'map' })}
          onFinished={() => finished(screen.yourTurn)}
        />
      );
    case 'stickers':
      return <StickerBook pack={services.pack} stickers={stickers} onBack={() => setScreen({ name: 'map' })} />;
  }
}
```

### Task 24: Installable and offline

Spec §11: installable to the home screen, full screen, landscape, offline. The icons are Buddy's head on paper, rendered from SVG to PNG once (`npm run icons`, committed): 180 px for iOS, 192 and 512 for the manifest, and a maskable 512 with Buddy inside Android's safe circle. vite-plugin-pwa writes the manifest and a service worker that caches everything on first load — the app, the fonts (Latin only: about 1.1 MB in all, inside §11's 5 MB), every picture, and the voice clips once they exist. `npm run dev:tablet` serves over https on the local network, which the tablet needs: `crypto.randomUUID` (profiles) and the service worker work only in a secure context (Plan 1A's notes).

**Files:**
- Create: `public/icons/icon.svg`, `public/icons/maskable.svg`, `scripts/make-icons.ts`
- Create (generated): `public/icons/apple-touch-icon.png`, `icon-192.png`, `icon-512.png`, `maskable-512.png`
- Modify: `vite.config.ts`, `index.html`, `package.json` (scripts `icons`, `dev:tablet`)

```ts
// scripts/make-icons.ts
// Renders the app icons (home screen, install, favicon) from public/icons/icon.svg and maskable.svg.
// Usage: npm run icons   (the PNGs are committed; run again after changing the SVGs)
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
import { Resvg } from '@resvg/resvg-js';

const ICONS = new URL('../public/icons/', import.meta.url);
const read = (file: string) => readFileSync(new URL(file, ICONS), 'utf8');
const icon = read('icon.svg');
const maskable = read('maskable.svg');

const out: Array<[file: string, svg: string, size: number]> = [
  ['apple-touch-icon.png', icon, 180], // iOS home screen
  ['icon-192.png', icon, 192],
  ['icon-512.png', icon, 512],
  ['maskable-512.png', maskable, 512], // Android crops it to its own shape: Buddy stays inside the safe circle
];
for (const [file, svg, size] of out) {
  writeFileSync(new URL(file, ICONS), new Resvg(svg, { fitTo: { mode: 'width', value: size } }).render().asPng());
}
console.log(`${out.length} icons written to public/icons`);
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Make it installable and offline**

**File: `vite.config.ts`**

```ts
import { defineConfig } from 'vitest/config';
import react from '@vitejs/plugin-react';
import basicSsl from '@vitejs/plugin-basic-ssl';
import { VitePWA } from 'vite-plugin-pwa';

// GitHub Pages serves a project site under /<repo>/; the deploy workflow sets BASE_PATH. The app builds URLs as
// import.meta.env.BASE_URL + 'art/…', so the base must end in a slash.
const basePath = process.env.BASE_PATH || '/';

export default defineConfig(({ mode }) => ({
  base: basePath.endsWith('/') ? basePath : `${basePath}/`,
  plugins: [
    react(),
    // npm run dev:tablet — https on the local network, which the tablet needs (crypto.randomUUID, the service worker)
    ...(mode === 'tablet' ? [basicSsl()] : []),
    // spec §11: installable, full screen, offline; every picture, font and voice clip is cached on first load
    ...(process.env.VITEST ? [] : [VitePWA({
      registerType: 'autoUpdate',
      manifest: {
        name: 'Buddy',
        short_name: 'Buddy',
        description: 'A learning game for Lower KG, with Buddy the baby elephant',
        lang: 'en',
        display: 'fullscreen',
        orientation: 'landscape',
        background_color: '#f6efe2',
        theme_color: '#f6efe2',
        start_url: '.',
        scope: '.',
        icons: [
          { src: 'icons/icon-192.png', sizes: '192x192', type: 'image/png' },
          { src: 'icons/icon-512.png', sizes: '512x512', type: 'image/png' },
          { src: 'icons/maskable-512.png', sizes: '512x512', type: 'image/png', purpose: 'maskable' },
        ],
      },
      workbox: {
        globPatterns: ['**/*.{js,css,html,svg,png,woff2,json,mp3}'],
        navigateFallback: 'index.html',
      },
    })]),
  ],
  build: { chunkSizeWarningLimit: 1500 },
  test: {
    environment: 'node',
    globals: true, // lets @testing-library/react clean up after each test; tests still import from vitest
    include: ['src/**/*.test.ts', 'src/**/*.test.tsx', 'scripts/**/*.test.ts'],
  },
}));
```

### Task 25: Publishing on GitHub Pages

The workflow checks, builds under `/<repository>/` and publishes on every push to `main` (spec §11, decision: GitHub Pages). Creating the repository, choosing whether it is private, and switching Pages on are the parent's steps — the README says how, and what a public repository would show: the docs describe the child.

**Files:**
- Create: `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`
- Modify: `README.md`

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the workflow**

**File: `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`**

```yaml
# Builds and publishes Buddy to GitHub Pages on every push to main (spec §11: a web app, installable, offline).
name: Deploy to GitHub Pages

on:
  push:
    branches: [main]
  workflow_dispatch:

permissions:
  contents: read
  pages: write
  id-token: write

concurrency:
  group: pages
  cancel-in-progress: true

jobs:
  build:
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    steps:
      - uses: actions/checkout@v7
      - uses: actions/setup-node@v7
        with:
          node-version: 24
          cache: npm
      - run: npm ci
      - run: npm run check
      - run: npm run build
        env:
          # a project site lives under /<repo>/
          BASE_PATH: /${{ github.event.repository.name }}/
      - uses: actions/configure-pages@v6
      - uses: actions/upload-pages-artifact@v5
        with:
          path: dist
  deploy:
    needs: build
    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
    environment:
      name: github-pages
      url: ${{ steps.deployment.outputs.page_url }}
    steps:
      - id: deployment
        uses: actions/deploy-pages@v5
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Rewrite the README**

**File: `README.md`**

```markdown
# Buddy — an interactive learning game for Lower KG

A touch-first web app for a child aged 4–5: look after Buddy the baby elephant through the day (habits, manners, feelings) and play mini-games together on a map covering the *My First Steps with Cambridge* Lower KG syllabus.

- **Design specification:** [docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-buddy-learning-game-design.md](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-01-buddy-learning-game-design.md)
- **Plans:** [the engine (1A)](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-01-plan-1a-m1-engine.md) · [play (1B)](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-06-plan-1b-m1-play.md)
- **Status:** the engine (Plan 1A) and the playable Map — all fifteen M1 games, Buddy, celebrations and stickers (Plan 1B) — are built; Buddy's Home, the feelings check-in, the session timer and the parent corner (Plan 1C) are next.

## Engine (Plan 1A)

Framework-free TypeScript under `src/engine/`; content as JSON under `content/`.

| Command | What it does |
|---|---|
| `npm test` | every test (Vitest) |
| `npm run typecheck` | `tsc --noEmit`, then the app alone without Node types (`tsconfig.app.json`) |
| `npm run validate` | cross-reference and rule checks on the content pack, and every picture it names (`--strict` fails on warnings) |
| `npm run inspect -- <templateId> [--seed N] [--level 1\|2\|3]` | print the set a seed and level make with no history (a goal's first set) |
| `npm run voice -- [--dry-run] [--prune]` | generate voice clips with Azure Speech (needs `.env`) |
| `npm run check` | typecheck + tests + validate |

Content lives in `content/*.json` and `content/templates/*.json`; adding a game is adding a template and, if needed, pool items and lines. Run `npm run validate` after every content change.

## The app (Plan 1B)

React under `src/app/`, on the engine: an installable web app that works offline after its first load.

| Command | What it does |
|---|---|
| `npm run dev` | the app at http://localhost:5173 |
| `npm run dev:tablet` | the app over https on the local network, for trying it on the tablet (accept the self-signed certificate once); it does not install or work offline — the published build does |
| `npm run build` / `npm run preview` | the production build in `dist/`, and serving it |
| `npm run art` | fetch the OpenMoji pictures listed in `art/openmoji.json` into `public/art/` (see `art/README.md`) |
| `npm run icons` | render the app icons from `public/icons/*.svg` |

Buddy speaks every line with the device's voice until the voice clips are generated (`npm run voice`).

### Publishing on GitHub Pages

The workflow `.github/workflows/deploy.yml` checks, builds and publishes the app on every push to `main`.

1. Decide what the repository shows. Its `docs/` describe the child (age, school, how they learn). Either keep the repository private — GitHub Pages from a private repository needs a paid GitHub plan — or publish from a repository without those documents.
2. Create the repository on GitHub, add it as the `origin` remote, and push `main`.
3. In the repository's **Settings → Pages**, set **Source** to **GitHub Actions**. The next push (or **Actions → Deploy to GitHub Pages → Run workflow**) publishes `https://<user>.github.io/<repository>/`.
4. On the tablet, open that address and add it to the home screen (Safari: **Share → Add to Home Screen**) before setting Buddy up: on an iPad the home-screen app keeps its own data, apart from Safari's. Open it from the home screen once while online (it saves itself for offline then) and set the child up there. It opens full screen and works offline.
5. After a push, the tablet picks up the new version in two steps: open Buddy once while online, then close it (swipe it away) and open it again.

Nothing in the app leaves the device (spec §10): no accounts, no analytics, no requests except the app's own files.

The pictures are OpenMoji and drawings in its style, under CC BY-SA 4.0 ([art/README.md](art/README.md)); the rest of the repository is not licensed for reuse (`UNLICENSED`).
```

**File: `docs/superpowers/checklists/2026-10-06-m1-play-tablet.md`**

```markdown
# M1 play — tablet and playtest checklist

For the parent, before and during the first playtests of Plan 1B (spec §14: devices and playtests, a manual checklist per milestone). Use the published address, or `npm run dev:tablet` on the same Wi-Fi for everything but Install and Offline (the development server has no service worker).

## On the tablet (iPad Safari, then an Android tablet in Chrome)

- [ ] **Install.** Before setting Buddy up, Share → Add to Home Screen, and set up in the app it adds (on an iPad it keeps its own data, apart from Safari's): the icon shows Buddy; it opens full screen.
- [ ] **No zoom.** A two-finger pinch on the Map or in a game doesn't zoom the app.
- [ ] **First run.** The setup screen takes the child's name with the on-screen keyboard; the Buddy beside it changes colour and accessory as you choose; a suggested name is spoken when tapped; Return on the keyboard closes it without starting.
- [ ] **Hello.** Tapping Buddy says the whole greeting out loud, from its first word ("Good morning, <name>!") to its last, before Buddy's suggestion on the Map; on the Android tablet, try it right after opening the app too. If it is silent: the volume, the mute switch, then Safari's console (via a Mac) — it logs `speech voice: …`, the voice the device chose. A novelty voice (Albert, Bad News…) or a network voice means `pickVoice` should prefer local voices.
- [ ] **The next day.** Leave Buddy open on the home screen overnight, then open it again: does Hello greet the child again? If not, Buddy should go back to Hello after a long time away (Plan 1C).
- [ ] **The Map.** Five islands scroll sideways; Buddy points at one spot and says so; any spot can be tapped; the sticker book opens from its number too.
- [ ] **Every game.** Each of the ten spots plays a set to the celebration. In each: the prompt is spoken and 🔊 repeats it; a wrong answer gives "try again" and a wiggle; a second gives "It's this one!" and the answer; after 8 quiet seconds the prompt repeats. In the kindness scenes, Buddy's reaction ("That was kind! …") is heard to the end before the next scene starts.
- [ ] **Dragging.** Items follow the finger smoothly, without the page scrolling; tapping an item then its place works too, even a slow, slightly wobbly tap; matching draws a line that stays.
- [ ] **Letters.** In *Capital ↔ small*, capital **I** and small **l** look different; **a** and **g** are the single-storey shapes the school uses.
- [ ] **Celebration.** Confetti, the sticker flies into the book in the corner with a whoosh you can hear, Buddy dances to music, all smoothly; every second or third set Buddy says "Your turn!" and counts to three, each number appearing as he says it, cheers, then asks "Again?" as its button appears — once. Tapping Buddy skips it.
- [ ] **Sticker book.** Each sticker is on its island's page; shiny ones glow.
- [ ] **Leaving.** The 🏠 button mid-set goes back to the Map. Locking the screen mid-prompt stops the voice.
- [ ] **Sound after a lock.** Lock the screen mid-set, unlock, answer: the chime still sounds, Buddy's voice says the next prompt, and the next dance has its music. The stomp's thump can be heard on the tablet's own speaker.
- [ ] **Offline.** After one full visit, turn on airplane mode, close the app and open it again from the home screen: it starts and plays.
- [ ] **Layout.** Nothing is cut off or overlapping in landscape; the corner Buddy never covers an answer (watch tap-all at level 3, with eight cards, and the first slot of a four- or five-step routine). Portrait and a phone are usable, if cramped (the phone pass is M4).

## Playtest with the child (spec §1.2, §14)

- [ ] Starts a visit alone: taps Buddy, then a spot.
- [ ] Understands the prompts without reading. Note any game where the child waits for the repeat, or taps at random.
- [ ] Plays a game again willingly; note favourites and ones avoided.
- [ ] "Your turn" lands: does the child copy Buddy?
- [ ] Frustration points: a game, a picture nobody recognises, a word said unclearly. Pictures to watch: *share* (two hands with a biscuit — may read as "please"), *roti* (a striped disc), *crayons* (one crayon); of those drawn for Buddy, *nail cutter*, *handkerchief*, and *mat* (rolled up) and *rug* (fringed), which can meet in a rhyming game.
- [ ] Levels: how many sets a game takes to move up (it needs a set and the one before it at 85% together). Note any game that never rises, or rises too soon.
- [ ] Tap-all's first hint wiggles every answer still to find, and so does the plate's: does that give the round away?
- [ ] Matching capital and small letters: picking a letter says its name, and both halves of a pair have the same name, so a round can be matched by ear alone. Does the child look at the letters? If not, Buddy should say the name only once the pair is joined.
- [ ] Counting groups of seven to ten (level 3) draw each picture small: can the child still count them?
- [ ] Matching by tapping: does the child tap a picked card again, to hear its word or to count a group? That puts it down, so the next tap picks a card instead of joining the pair. If this keeps happening, a tap on the picked card should keep it picked.
- [ ] Giving a number: does the child count Buddy's copies by touching them? A touch takes a copy back; if this keeps happening, taking back should become a drag.
- [ ] Buddy's faces in the feelings scenes: can the child say which is sad, scared and angry?
- [ ] Stickers from the feelings game can be a sad, angry or scared face: does the child mind such a reward?
- [ ] Answering by elimination in the kindness scenes ("laugh at them", "push to the front" are always wrong): does the child choose the kind answer, or just avoid the odd one?
- [ ] An unkind choice makes the friend's face sadder: in *one biscuit* and *friend crying* the friend then sobs (😭). Is that too much for the child? If so, those scenes should start from a calmer face.
- [ ] While a feelings scene plays, Buddy keeps his usual small smile, even as his ice cream falls: does the child answer "happy" from his face?
- [ ] The kindness choices are pictures with their names written under them, never said: does the child know what each one shows? If not, Buddy should name each choice as it appears.
- [ ] Buddy says his suggestion each time the Map opens, even back from the sticker book: does the child follow it, or tire of it?

Note anything odd with the time and the game: every round can be replayed from its record (`replayRound`, spec §14).
```

## Notes for Plan 1C

- **Home comes before the Map** (§4.1, §4.2): Hello → Home (due routine steps; the door opens when they are done) → check-in at the door → the Map. In 1B, Hello opens the Map, and the game's 🏠 button returns to the Map; in 1C it returns home, and the Map gets a door back to Home.
- **Routine stickers** (§4.6) use the sticker table as sets do, with the scene id as `source`: `pageOf` already files an unknown source on the Home page, which the sticker book does not show yet.
- **The session ends** (§4.1, §4.4): `Settings.timerMinutes`, Buddy's yawn at T−2 min and the "Buddy is at school / asleep" screen are 1C's; nothing in 1B ends a session. `roundsToPlay` already cuts sets to five rounds after 10 minutes. An app left open overnight carries on yesterday's visit, with no greeting and the same session: go back to Hello after a long time hidden. The wind-down (or Home's door) also leads back to Hello, which is how a second child plays (1B needs a reload); count "your turn" per visit.
- **The parent gate** (§8) should cover Hello's ➕ (adding a child) as well as the parent corner. The corner's quiet storage warning reads `services.storage.failures`. Progress shows `goalStatus` with `formSummary` beside it ("shaky" in 1 of 3 game types reads as learning, not failing), and named confusions come from `AttemptDetails.wrongIds`.
- **The record clock** (`lastRecordedAt`) starts after a profile's latest attempt or set result. 1B's stickers always follow a set result of the same visit; 1C stamps routine events, check-ins and routine stickers, often in visits with no attempt, so extend `lastRecordedAt` to every table the clock stamps. It scans backwards from the latest record of any profile until it finds this one's: fine at M1's size, but a `[profileId+at]` index on `attempts` and `setResults` (Dexie `version(2)`; the `startSession` query pattern) makes it constant — cheapest before the first deploy or with the next schema change.
- **Reading wrong picks** (`AttemptDetails.wrongIds`) needs the attempt's mechanic: an option's id (tap, tap-all, scenes; for letters, a glyph id, so b/d shows directly), `item>target` (sort, sequence, match; a sequence's target is `slot:<i>` counted from 0, shown as i + 1; a size copy is `<object>@s` or `@l` in a sort, so `log@l>small` is the big log in the small basket, and `<object>@<i>` in a tap, `i` its place among the scales from the smallest; in a match, `from` is the card the child started from, on either side, so read `from>to` as an unordered pair, a counted group being `<object>x<n>` and a numeral `n<k>`), a junk item given (the plate), or `n:<count>` (give a number). Calendar and "last played" views should use `Session.startedAt` (wall clock), not `at`: after a clock change `at` keeps after the latest record until real time catches up.
- **Scrub and dress-buddy** belong to the Home scenes, not to templates; they can still use `useRoundFlow` for hints and `useDnd` for dragging. Spec §4.4's cues (`cue-hint`, `cue-show`) and `ItemView` are shared.
- **Storage failures** (§13): 1B counts a failed or stalled storage call (`StorageHealth`; a call gives up after `STORAGE_WAIT_MS`) and drops the record, so while storage keeps failing this visit's rounds don't feed variety or levels. Keeping the visit's records in memory for the history builders, as §13 has it, is 1C's if wanted. A visit whose session couldn't be started records its rounds under session 1, so if storage recovers mid-visit the next visit's variety is a little off.
- **Sticker pages**: `pageOf` files a sticker by its template's topic, and an unknown template under `'home'`, which 1B's book doesn't show: keep template ids stable (renaming one would hide its stickers), or file stickers by topic (§4.6: nothing is ever removed).
- **The Map's suggestion** (`suggestTopic` in `picker.ts`) counts a topic with any template, while the Map shows only topics with a game the app can play (`islandSpots`). They agree in M1, where every topic has one; once a topic has only Home or unbuilt games, pass the Map's topics to `suggestTopic`, or Buddy points at nothing.
- **Names** live in two places, `services.speaker.names` and the visit's names in `App.tsx`: renaming Buddy (the wardrobe, §4.7) must change both. Setup is the only guard against an empty child's name; a profile restored without one would caption a greeting "Good evening, !".
- **Settings** in the parent corner: music on/off (`Music` and `Sfx` take no mute flag yet), TTS fallback, reduced motion (1B honours only the system setting; add the `reduced-motion` class on the root, which `buddy.css` and `theme.css` already check).
- **The setup tip** says to add Buddy to the home screen first (an iPad's home-screen app keeps its own data, apart from Safari's): show it only outside the installed app (`matchMedia('(display-mode: standalone)')`, or `navigator.standalone` on iOS).
- **The OpenMoji credit** (CC BY-SA 4.0) is on the setup screen, below the fold on a tablet: the parent corner should show it too.
- **Device voices**: `pickVoice` should prefer a voice with `localService`: an online voice (some of Chrome's) sends the spoken lines, the child's name among them, to its provider, and fails offline.
- **The school's initials** reach the public build in two templates' `examFormat` (`dps-numeracy-3`, `dps-numeracy-7`; nothing reads them): rename them to something neutral if the repository or site is public.
- **Voice clips on the iPad**: Howler plays clips through an `<audio>` element (`html5: true`), which Safari loads with a `Range` request, and Workbox's precache answers ranges with a full 200, which Safari may refuse. Before the clips ship, play one on the iPad from the home-screen app, online and offline. If it fails: `html5: false` (Web Audio, the whole file), or `injectManifest` with a range-aware route ahead of `precacheAndRoute` (`createPartialResponse`).
- **Voice clips**: until `npm run voice` runs (with the parent's Azure key; inside the sandbox add `NODE_USE_ENV_PROXY=1`), every line is device speech. Plan 1B added `buddy.suggest` and `fb.your_turn_again`; regenerating covers them. The service worker caches the clips with the app once they exist. Howler plays them through an `<audio>` element, and Safari asks for media with Range headers, which Workbox's precache route ignores (Workbox's `RangeRequestsPlugin` handles them): after `npm run voice`, check on the iPad, offline, that clips play rather than fall back to device speech.
- **What the parent's checklist (Task 26) turns up**: the voice each tablet picks, drag feel, layout, and the playtest notes — how many sets a game takes to rise, games avoided, pictures not recognised.
- **Spec details Plan 1B leaves out**, each small: Buddy's occasional sneeze or yawn and looking toward the last tap (§4.7 idle life); celebrations draw only on the dances, each with its music, while §4.5 also allows the actions; the calm Home and livelier Map music loops (§7: 1B has only the dance loops); a haptic tick (§11; iOS Safari has no vibration API).
- **Open from Plan 1A, still open**: the drag-to-match arrangement has no slot rule (a two-pair round lines up straight across in half the rounds); distractors do not consult history; small pools repeat within a set at higher levels (grow them with the pictures); a three-bin sort can leave a bin empty; the generator history carries only the last slot, so an answer can sit in the same slot three rounds running across a set boundary: the last two rounds of a set and the first of the goal's next (3 times in 53 sets in a simulation of Task 13's review; spec §2 rule 2).
