## Execution notes (deviations from the task text, recorded as tasks complete)

The task text above is kept as written; where execution differs, the change is listed here, and the committed code is authoritative.

**Task 1 — scaffold**
- The code review found the parity test covers only Vitest's glob: Vite 8's production build globs with Rolldown's native plugin, so a later Vite could reorder the templates (and the seeded picks, and bridging's first match) while every test stays green. `src/app/pack.ts` sorts the template files itself, by code unit as `readRawPack` does, and says why.
- `vite.config.ts` ends `BASE_PATH` in a slash if it lacks one (`process.env.BASE_PATH || '/'`, so an empty value counts as unset): Vite adds the slash to asset URLs but not to `import.meta.env.BASE_URL`, from which the app builds every picture and clip URL — `/buddy` would have given `/buddyart/…`, missing pictures and a device voice, and no error.
- `"prebuild": "npm run validate"`: spec §14 says a build fails on content errors, and the pack is parsed only when the app starts, so a hand build of bad content gave a blank page. `engines.node` is `^22.22.2 || ^24.15.0 || >=26.0.0`, what jsdom 30 needs.

**Task 2 — two sets together**
- Three more assertions (still 11 tests): a set at ≤50% at level 1 also starts afresh and is never paired — `[6/6, 3/6, 6/6]` stays at 1, where resetting only on an actual fall (a natural tidy-up) would rise, in 46,417 M1-shaped histories; `[1/2, 10/10]` stays at 1; and an empty set is never the second of two (`[6/6, 0/0]` stays at 1 — without the guard, 0/0 paired with 6/6 rose). Mutation pass: all 32 mutants killed (28 under the task's tests). The doc comment says "a set at ≤50% … is never paired, at level 1 too" and "two sets of 4 or more rounds".
- Spec rule 7 corrected: pooling lets one slip through only in sets of 4 or more rounds; M1's 2- and 3-round games (`good-habits.order`, the two sorts, the two scene games) still need every round right, as before (their medians stay 3 and 4 sets, as the longer games' now are: 3–4). "Answered right before any hint" replaces "right-first-time" (an answer after the idle wiggle is `corrected`).

**Task 3 — wrong picks, stickers, record time**
- As written. The review's points went to later tasks (below) and to the notes for Plan 1C: `lastRecordedAt` scans back from the latest record of any profile (a `[profileId+at]` index would make it constant) and should cover every table the record clock stamps once 1C adds routine records.

**Task 4 — signposts, lines, the picture list**
- The review found the glyph check used `isGlyphAsset` to test itself (a broken one still passed): the test now asserts no `glyph:` path directly and tests `isGlyphAsset` on its own. A second test walks the whole raw content — every `asset` value and every `.svg` string, bar the vignettes — so a picture field added later that `assetPaths` forgets fails it. `TopicSchema.asset` takes `glyph:` with one or two letters only (`glyph:` alone, or a word, would put nothing or reading on the Map), with a schema test. Two more tests (348 in all by Task 23). Left as it is: the refine takes any one or two characters after `glyph:`, so `glyph: ` would pass and draw a blank signpost — harmless in hand-written content.

**Task 5 — the OpenMoji pictures**
- The review found `bins/basket-big.svg` and `basket-small.svg` byte-identical: the big/small sorting game showed two identical baskets. The small one is now a composition — the same basket drawn at 40 of 72, standing on the same baseline.
- Six listed pictures nothing used (`ui/sparkles`, `ui/party`, `parts/cloud`, `parts/sun`, `parts/bandage`, `parts/house`) are gone: 121 pictures, not 127.
- The composer refuses a drawing that is not 72 × 72 (each layer goes in a 72 × 72 box) or that refers to its own ids (stripping them would leave the reference pointing at nothing), and a file with anything before its `<svg>`. `art/openmoji.json` is checked entry by entry with Zod, so a typo or a missing number names its entry instead of writing `width="undefined"`. A fetch times out after 30 s and names the picture, with the proxy hint.
- New `scripts/art/pictures.test.ts`: every listed picture exists, each composition's boxes are its layers (an edited composition is rewritten only once its file is deleted), and no SVG in `public/art` — fetched or drawn — holds a script, an event handler, an embedded document, a link or an entity (Pages serves them from the app's own origin). 10 art tests instead of 5.
- A second round (the re-review): quoted id references (`url('#g')`) are refused too; a fetch whose body times out still names its picture; the pictures test also checks each composition's viewBox and shapes (an edited scene left stale) and catches a namespace-prefixed `script` element and `javascript:` links.
- Left as they are (optional, from the last check): the shapes check is a prefix, so dropping a scene's last shape from the json without regenerating still passes (ending the prefix with `<svg x="` would close it); and the proxy hint is added to an HTTP error too, where it points the wrong way for a 404.

**Task 6 — the drawn pictures**
- The review found two drawings a four-year-old would misread, and three more worth redoing. The handkerchief, a diamond crossed with lines, looked like a kite: it is now an upright cloth with a blue check and one corner folded over. The nail cutter looked like a stapler: it is now the familiar side view, jaws at the front, the lever pinned there and rising to the back, a ring hole at the rear. The rug, three nested ovals, looked like a plate or a target: it is now a carpet with a border, a diamond in the middle and fringes. The mat had looked like that carpet: it is now a striped mat rolled up at the back. Mat and rug can meet in a rhyming game, so they must not be confused. The cabbage, which could pass for a watermelon, has layered leaves. Smaller fixes: the towel's lower white stripe was hidden by its outlines and is now wider, and the ball's band now reaches its outline.
- `npm run validate` now compares picture names with a listing of the files in `public/art` instead of asking the file system. macOS ignores case and GitHub Pages does not, so `objects/Ball.svg` used to pass here and then show a blank picture on the tablet; a folder's name no longer passes either. The message says names are case-sensitive, and what to do: `npm run art` fetches a picture that `art/openmoji.json` lists; any other picture needs drawing, or its OpenMoji code listed.
- Left as they are: the drawn pictures are named in `art/README.md`'s prose, not in a list a test could read, and validate already tells a listed picture from an unlisted one. Their CC BY-SA line names no licensor: the licensor is the project's owner, and naming them should be settled with the repository and privacy decision (Task 25's README, step 1), before the repository goes public.

**Task 7 — the theme and the shared pieces**
- The review found that the hint and demonstration glow never reached an option. `.cue-hint` and `.option` are equally specific, and the mechanics' stylesheet loads later, so only bins and slots glowed. With Reduce Motion on, a hint on an option showed nothing at all. The cue classes are now doubled (`.cue-hint.cue-hint`), and the glow has a darker rim (`--glow-edge`), because yellow alone barely shows on the paper.
- Buttons lose the browser's own look (`background: none; border: 0; padding: 0`): in Chrome, the bare drag wrappers in sort, order and give showed a grey box.
- The paper grain is a 160 px PNG, drawn once from the same SVG noise. Safari re-runs a tiled SVG's filter on every repaint, and dragging repaints. The precache grows by one file.
- `.icon-button:active` now uses `scale`, and `tiltFor` returns a value for the `rotate` property. An inline `transform` tilt had cancelled the press feedback on every option, and an `:active` transform had cancelled Again's own scale.
- Nine is drawn 3×3 rather than 4+4+1, with pictures over a third bigger and no straggler. 4 to 8 already match the LKG worksheets. Ten stays 4+4+2, because a ten-frame's 5+5 would shrink its pictures to about 20 px.
- `Picture` remembers which asset failed, so an instance handed another picture tries it (the kindness scene's friend changes face mid-round). The progress dots are labelled "rounds played". `.reduced-motion` gets the media query's rule, ready for Plan 1C's setting.
- Three more tests, 11 in all: pictures follow the base path, a new asset is tried after a failure, and `useLater` drops its timers on unmount. The 🔊 test now checks that the button sits right before the caption, and the columns table includes nine.
- Left as it is: on a phone, a counted group's copies can come out at 0 px or less. That is M4's concern, and the card size is the real fix.

**Task 8 — Buddy**
- The review found that "transforms only, so they stay smooth" isn't true inside an SVG. Browsers don't composite an SVG's parts, so they repaint Buddy every frame a part moves. At the celebration's size, the dances and the jump moved the whole body inside the SVG and repainted all of Buddy each frame, under falling confetti. Those whole-body moves now animate the `<svg>` itself, which the browser can composite; their lift distances are rescaled to the SVG's box. The header says why.
- With reduced motion on, a move now keeps its key pose, held still: the point at the answer, the raised arms of a jump to copy, the hmm tilt. Before, Buddy stood still, so "It's this one!" pointed at nothing and "Can you jump like me?" showed nothing to copy.
- Smaller fixes:
  - Each leg's toenails are inside the leg, so they lift with it; the stomp used to leave them on the floor.
  - Sad's tear is bigger, and sits on the cheek clear of the glasses.
  - `isBuddyColour` uses `Object.hasOwn`, so `toString` from a corrupt stored profile is no colour.
  - Each accessory has its own class, and the test checks that the chosen one is drawn.
  - `move`'s doc says that replaying a move takes a new `key`.
- The same review found that a reaction repeated within its reset window doesn't replay, and that the first reaction's reset cuts the second short. For example, two quick wrong taps dropped Buddy's pointing arm after about 0.6 s while the answer still glowed. The fix is in the callers, Tasks 18 and 21 (below).
- Left as it is: the head's pivot shifts with the cap or scarf, because both sit inside the head's box. That is harmless at the 9° tilt; move the scarf out of the head if M2 turns the head further.

**Task 9 — the Speaker**
- The review found the tests left most of the Speaker unpinned. Each of five breakages still passed every test in the plan (357):
  - `clearPrompt()` doing nothing;
  - dropping the "Buddy" default, which an unnamed Buddy needs ("is hungry!");
  - not keeping a scenario's narration for 🔊;
  - `stop()` doing nothing;
  - `word()` sending another wording.

  New and stronger tests now catch each one (10 in the folder, not 8):
  - an unnamed Buddy is "Buddy" aloud and in the caption;
  - 🔊 replays a scenario's narration, and every narration of a scenario topic ends with its one-wording question;
  - `clearPrompt` forgets a prompt that was asked;
  - a test for `word()` and `stop()`.
- `fillNames` fills both names in one pass with a function, so a typed name goes in as typed: `$&`, `$'` and `{buddy}` in a name had been interpreted.
- The wording test is seeded (`mulberry32(1)`); with `Math.random` it failed about once in 19,000 runs. The failed-line test spies on `console.warn` instead of printing a stack trace.
- Left as they are:
  - `say()` still throws on an unknown line id: every id that reaches it is checked by a test, and the throw fails loudly there.
  - An empty child's name can't reach a line in 1B (see the notes for Plan 1C).
  - Replaying a scenario's whole narration is right for a child who can't read.

**Task 10 — synthesised effects and music**
- **The critical bug.** `createMusic` defaulted to `timers = { set: setTimeout, clear: clearTimeout }` and called `timers.set(…)`. In a browser, calling `setTimeout` as a method of another object throws "Illegal invocation". The music started, the throw then skipped the timer that ends the dance, and the celebration waited for two taps. Node and Vitest timers ignore `this`, so no test saw it. Music now calls `setTimeout` and `clearTimeout` directly; the parameter was unused. A test checks every call's `this`.
- **Sound after an interruption.** iOS can leave the audio context suspended or interrupted after a screen lock, an app switch or a call, and only Hello's tap resumed it. Every effect and the dance music then stayed silent for the rest of the visit, while the voice went on. The new `unlockOnEveryTap` resumes the context inside any tap. Task 21's `services.ts` installs it.
- **Stopping and timing.**
  - `stop()` cancels the notes of a queued loop that hasn't started. Before, the next loop's first beat sounded under the fade at the end of every dance, as loud as the music when the timer ran late.
  - A late loop timer starts the loop afresh, instead of sounding its past-due notes together.
  - A refused `AudioContext` gives silence, not an exception in Hello's tap.
  - The `Tone` bounds are documented.
- **Sound design, from the review's measurements.**
  - The miss "hmm" and the whoosh swell in, with a new `attack`. The "hmm" was a 100 ms "bup", and the whoosh was mostly inaudible.
  - The stomp's bass is an octave up as a triangle, and its thump is 220→90 Hz. Modelled through a tablet speaker, which loses everything below about 250 Hz, they had vanished. Task 26 checks this by ear. The whoosh is still the quietest effect, 10 dB under the chime: if it is faint on the iPad, raise its gain from 0.05 to about 0.08.
- **Tests.** Five more, 19 in the folder: tap resume, plain timers, the late loop, the cancelled queue, and a refused context. "Plays one dance at a time" now shows the first dance stopping.
- **Left as it is.** On Android, Chrome keeps the context running in the background. A celebration whose dance starts just after the child switches apps would play its music unseen. A visibility check before `music.play` in Task 21 would cover it.

**Task 11 — the voice in the browser**
- The review approved the task as written and found its premises sound against the sources:
  - Howler 2.2.4 registers its unlock listeners when the first Howl is built, even one with `preload: false`.
  - WebKit lifts the gesture requirement for speech on any `speak()` in a tap, including one with empty text.
  - The empty utterance can't delay the greeting, because the greeting's own `stop()` cancels it in the same tick.
- Hardening:
  - `loadManifest` gives up after 3 s. The app waits for the manifest before its first screen, so a stalled request on a first visit (no service worker yet) left a blank page.
  - `primeHowler` and `unlockSpeech` catch their own errors. On a 48 kHz tablet Howler replaces its first AudioContext; if the browser refused the second, `new Howl()` threw out of `createServices` and the app showed "Buddy could not start".
  - `tts.ts` exports `PREFERRED_LANGS`, so the voice the log names is the one the app uses, even when the preferences change after the tablet check.
- Four more tests, 26 in the folder:
  - clip URLs under the base path;
  - `primeHowler`, which builds one Howl and survives a throw;
  - `unlockSpeech`, which speaks one silent utterance, synchronously;
  - `primeVoices`, which waits for `voiceschanged`.
  
  The fallback test adds bad JSON, `clips: null` and a slow manifest.
- Left as it is: the `as unknown as HowlCtor` cast, which is unnecessary but harmless.

**Task 12 — the clock, the write queue, the feedback rules**
- The review found that a storage write that never settles blocked every later set. Safari's IndexedDB has been seen to stall after the app resumes, and this is what followed:
  - `idle()` never came, so the next set's history never loaded;
  - the child got an empty game screen, from every island, with no suggestion on the Map;
  - every later write queued behind the stuck one;
  - and no failure was counted, so the parent would see no warning.

  `within()` now gives up on a storage call after `STORAGE_WAIT_MS` (3 s) and counts it as failed, the way speech is never waited on. `guarded` uses it for writes, and Tasks 13 and 23 use it for reads (below): a stalled database also holds reads.
- Miss counts read only the state's own keys (`Object.hasOwn`). An item id like `constructor` would otherwise have read Object.prototype and shown the answer on the first miss. No content id clashes today.
- Three more tests, 13 in the folder:
  - a write with no answer is given up, and the next one runs;
  - a key named after Object.prototype counts from nought;
  - one run that pins five reachable slips the tests let through. A wiggle is a hint but not a miss and adds no wrong pick, a third miss shows again, and a wiggle after a demonstration leaves the round `shown`.

  The clock's doc says `at` orders records and is not a date.
- Left as they are:
  - If storage stalls completely, each set waits about 9 s (three capped reads).
  - A write cut off by iOS suspending the page can count as a failure when the page resumes.
  - `hinted` duplicates `hints > 0`.

**Task 13 — the set runner and stickers**
- The review found the code right, and checked it beyond its tests:
  - Every engine call uses the right key and session.
  - A simulation of 4 sessions × 16 sets, a quarter of them left early, replayed all 226 attempts and never repeated a scene twice running.
  - Every sticker in every template and level is a picture the book can draw.
  - A year of history reads in 66 ms.
- The tests let wrong versions pass. Each of these still passed the 9 tests:
  - counting a `corrected` round as correct;
  - recording level 1 or `at: 0`;
  - reading the history by template, or under the wrong session;
  - stickers from the unkind choices.

  Four more tests close these gaps, for 26 in the folder:
  - a set's result read back, with only `correct` rounds counted and its `at` after its rounds';
  - no result for a set left early;
  - each set's history and replay, through a set left early;
  - stickers never fall back to the stand-in, and show only kind acts, clean things and what was counted.

  The rotation test also reads back each game's levels across a new session.
- Two small code changes:
  - `prepareSet` reads the levels and the history together, so a stalled storage holds a set for 9 s, not 12.
  - `finishSet` records a result only for a set whose every round was played. Before, the caller alone guarded it, and a partial set would have been judged as a whole one.
- Left as they are:
  - `readOr` repeats `guarded`, and App.tsx has a third copy.
  - Small test nits.

**Task 14 — useRoundFlow**
- The review found the hook correct: one quiet timer, every guard reading the rules' state, nothing waiting on speech. Its tests, though, let six plausible slips pass, along with the whole plan's 378 tests:
  - a touch that never re-arms the quiet spell;
  - a touch that doesn't reset the repeats;
  - only one wiggle ever;
  - other timings than 8 s and 15 s;
  - a cheer said for a shown round;
  - misses not counted per key.

  Four more tests close these gaps, for 38 in the folder: the quiet-spell schedule in literal times, the per-key show, the page hidden, and ending before speaking. The show test now also checks that no cheer is said.
- `done()` ends the round, through `setEnded` and `onEnd`, before it speaks. A line that can't be said used to leave the round unfinished for good. The loud throw Task 9 chose stays.
- The quiet spell holds while the page is hidden. On Android and desktop the timers kept running: the prompt was said to a hidden page, and each wiggle counted a hint nobody saw, which made the answer `corrected`.
- The quiet spell starts when a round mounts. That is before the child can act in a scene, whose choices appear after 4.5 s, and in the first round of a bridged set. The fix is in Tasks 20 and 21 (below); `touch()`'s doc says so.
- Left as they are:
  - The 🔊 button doesn't restart the quiet spell, so the 8 s repeat can cut into a 🔊 replay. It's a Task 21 and playtest matter.
  - The options ref is written during render, which is safe here.

**Task 15 — tap-the-one and tap-all**
- The review found two ways a child's taps were miscounted.
  - **Taps went on counting while Buddy showed the answer.** In tap-the-one, a third wrong tap said "It's this one!" again and added a wrong pick. In tap-all, a tick by the child made the round end before Buddy finished, and a wrong tap scheduled a second show. Taps now wait once Buddy shows.
  - **A double tap on one wrong card counted as two misses.** That gave "try again" and "It's this one!" at once, and the round was recorded `shown` without the hint-and-retry of §4.4. A tap on the card that just shook is now the same answer again for `SETTLE_MS` (500 ms, new in useRoundFlow). The same card tapped later still counts as the second miss.

  Tasks 18 and 20 get the same two guards (below).
- Two more tests, 8 in the folder, pin both behaviours. The `options()` helper now looks each button up on use: a shaking card is a new element, and a click on the old one reaches nothing.
- The test fakes behave like the real speaker. `say` throws on an unknown line, and speech never settles, so a mechanic that waited on speech would now fail its tests.
- Pictures never go below 48 px. On a phone they used to vanish or take negative sizes; the phone layout as a whole is still M4's. The cards are sized to centre their picture, and the unused `.is-faded` style is gone.
- Left as it is: GiveToBuddy and SceneThenChoose have no size floor for a phone in portrait. That is M4's.

**Task 16 — drag, or tap then tap**
- The review found that a second touch could leave an item stuck, and with it the round. Three ways:
  - a second finger down during a drag;
  - a second finger tapping during a drag;
  - a resting finger lifting while another drags.

  `release` cleared the live drag before checking the pointer, and a new press replaced a live drag without putting its item back. The item kept `is-dragging`, which turns pointer events off, so it couldn't be tapped or dragged again. Misses count per item, so Buddy never placed it either. The pointer is now checked first. While an item still on the page is being dragged, a new press is ignored. A press that only rested, or whose item went mid-drag, can be taken over.
- A real tap picked twice. The click after pointerup always passed the `!drag.current` guard. A `press` ref now tells a tap, a drag and a click with no press before it (the keyboard, tests) apart. Task 19's matching game has the same pattern and says the word on pick, so the child heard each picture's name start, stop and start again. It gets the same fix (below).
- Two smaller fixes:
  - The drop target is looked up while the dragged item still ignores the pointer.
  - Only a mouse's main button drags.
- The tests caught 1 of 12 slips in the hook, for example `is-dragging` never removed, or `enabled` ignored. There are now five more tests (17 in the folder) and new assertions; they catch all 19 mutants of the fixed hook.
- Left as they are:
  - A selection can outlive its item (harmless: the mechanics ignore it).
  - The tray recentres under a drag when Buddy removes another item (cosmetic).
  - The selected item has no `aria-pressed`.
  - For the tablet check: if dragging stutters on the iPad, try `will-change: transform` on `.draggable.is-dragging` first.

**Task 17 — sort-into-bins and sequence**
- **The item Buddy shows took input.** During the 1.6 s before Buddy put it in, the child could drag that item or card again.
  - A third wrong drop said "It's this one!" a second time, cutting off the first, and recorded the confusion three times.
  - A right drop beat Buddy's hand.

  The item Buddy is showing now waits for him; the other items stay free, because the child carries on. Task 19's matching game had the same fault and gets the same lock (below). Tasks 15, 18 and 20 already lock.
- **Routine slots wrapped.** On a 10.2" iPad the routine's slots broke onto a second line: four cards at 1080 and 1024 px wide, five at every size. The size formula left out the arrows between the slots. It now counts them, so 3–5 slots stay on one row. This was measured in Chrome with the real components and CSS.
- **Tests.** Nine of fourteen slips passed the three tests. Among them:
  - Buddy's show ending the round;
  - Sequence's Buddy never placing the card;
  - a filled slot counting a miss;
  - wrong wiggle targets;
  - nothing drawn in the bins.

  There are now six tests (23 in the folder), and they catch 19 of 22 mutants. The frozen-round check had compared the order with itself; it now keeps a copy.
- **Smaller changes.**
  - Sort's placed check reads only its own keys (`Object.hasOwn`). An item id such as `constructor` would never have shown, and the round could never have ended.
  - Sort's pictures never go below 48 px.
- **Left as they are.**
  - Bins and slots can't be reached from a keyboard, and a filled slot doesn't name its card.
  - At 1180×820, a pulsing bin's glow is clipped by a few pixels at the bottom edge.
  - Two suggestions: also glow the item Buddy moves, and ignore drops on the slot Buddy is filling.

**Task 18 — give-to-Buddy**
- The review found five things a child would meet:
  - **Tap-then-tap onto the pile swapped instead of giving.** The tap on a given copy took it back, and the click then bubbled to the drop, which gave the picked copy, so the count stayed the same. A given copy now takes itself back only when nothing is picked up.
  - **Buddy on the stage never cheered, thought or pointed.** The game hides its corner Buddy for this mechanic, and the flow's reactions went only there. Buddy on the stage now cheers a right answer, thinks at a wrong count and points in a show. On the plate a junk food's "yuck" stays the reaction to a miss.
  - **The second wrong count's show pointed at the child's wrong copies**, then swapped them all at once. It now points at Buddy's answer: the child's own copies up to the number, topped up from the tray. Then it keeps exactly those.
  - **The quiet-spell wiggle never pointed at ✓.** With the right number given, it landed on Buddy, inviting more. Once something is given, it now points at ✓.
  - **On the plate, food landed beside the plate.** The prompt says "on the plate", but the plate stayed empty. Food given is now drawn on it (at most four, in a 2×2).
- Smaller fixes:
  - ✓ keeps its place, hidden, until something is given, and the given copies have a fixed width, so Buddy doesn't jump aside as copies come.
  - Pictures never go below 48 px.
- Five more tests, 32 in the folder: the plate's yuck, hint, show and lock; the ✓ bounce and the second wrong count's show; a tap on the pile; Buddy's reactions on the stage; the quiet-spell targets. The plan's four tests are unchanged. The tests now catch 31 of 35 mutants, against 6 of 24 before.
- Left as they are:
  - The drop is a `role="button"` holding real buttons (nested interactive).
  - The counting hint is only visual. Saying the numbers (`glyph.num.*`) is a suggestion.

**Task 19 — drag-to-match**
- The commit's trailer named "Claude Opus 5.5", copied from the task text. The controller amended it to name the implementer, so it is now eb88253, with the same tree.
- **A second touch.** The review found that a second touch could steal or end a drag: `onPointerDown` had no guard while a drag was going on, and the pointer's up and cancel never checked the pointer id.
  - In Chrome, with real touches, a palm landing on another card and moving 10 px turned a right drag into "try again". It recorded a miss (`fan>box`) and downgraded the round.
  - A press now ends only with its own pointer, and a new press is ignored while a line is being drawn, as in `useDnd`. Only a mouse's main button presses.
- **A drag and a tap's pick.** A drag kept what a tap had picked. The matched card then swallowed the next tap, which said its name but didn't pick it. A drag now puts down the tapped pick.
- **Tests.** The four tests caught 14 of 36 mutants. Five more tests (41 in the folder) bring that to 35 of 41:
  - a drag from either side draws a line that stays, and every non-drop draws nothing;
  - the lock while Buddy shows, which ends at `SHOW_MS`;
  - two fingers, in both lift orders;
  - a drag clearing a pick;
  - the quiet spell.
- **Layout.** Pictures never go below 48 px. In Chrome, the lines meet their cards' facing edges exactly after matches, Buddy's line and a rotation. At 1080–1180 px wide with four pairs, cards are 131–144 px and the corner Buddy clears them.
- **Left as they are:**
  - `aria-pressed` means "matched", not "picked".
  - The line colours are hard-coded.
- **For the parent** (in Task 26's playtest list, below):
  - Capital and small letters share a name, so a round can be matched by ear alone.
  - Counting groups of 7–10 draw each copy at 22–26 px.
  - A second tap on the picked card puts it down, so a child who taps again to hear the word, or to count a group, may pick a new card instead of joining the pair. If that keeps happening, the tap should keep the card picked.

**Task 20 — scene-then-choose and the registry**
- **The friend's face after an unkind choice.** The review found that the lesson's sad face (spec §6 #22) didn't show in two of the four kindness scenes.
  - In *one biscuit*, the friend starts sad (😢), and "eat it all alone" set that same face.
  - In *friend crying*, the friend starts sobbing (😭), and "laugh at them" turned that into one tear. After being laughed at, the friend looked less upset.
  - An unkind choice now makes the face one step sadder (`SADDER`: happy or hurt becomes sad, and sad becomes crying). *Friend crying* starts with one tear (😢, OpenMoji's "crying face").
  - The four scenes now go: friend crying 😢 → 😭 → 😊; one biscuit 😢 → 😭 → 😊; slide queue 😊 → 😢 → 😊; friend fell 🤕 → 😢 → 😊.
  - A test checks that every kindness scene's unkind choice makes the face sadder.
- **Tests.** The six tests caught 9 of 23 mutants:
  - judging by slot instead of `correct` passed, because only *angry* was tried in the broken-toy scene;
  - the check that Buddy never acts out a wrong feeling used a scene without Buddy in it.

  Ten tests (51 in the folder) catch 24 of 25 mutants. They cover:
  - both right answers, with Buddy acting out the one chosen;
  - a wrong feeling leaving Buddy neutral, with the hint wiggling only the right card;
  - no choices before `WATCH_MS`;
  - the quiet spell timed from when the choices appear;
  - a bounce counting once, nothing taken while Buddy shows, and the round ending exactly at `SHOW_MS`;
  - every feeling a scene asks for being one Buddy can show;
  - the registry: `scrub` isn't playable, and `Mechanic` throws for it.
- **Smaller changes.**
  - The choices' pictures never go below 48 px, as in every other mechanic.
  - `isPlayable` uses `Object.hasOwn`.
  - A comment on the lightning keeps its flash rate safe. It flickers twice a second over about 132 px, under WCAG 2.3.1's limit of three.
- **Checked and fine:**
  - In Chrome, reduced motion keeps every story: the ice cream ends on the ground, the gift and the biscuit pop in, and the lightning simply appears.
  - From 1024 to 1366 px wide, the pictures are 146–153 px, nothing is cut off, and the corner Buddy covers no card.
  - Every narration's question has begun by 4.2 s, before the choices appear at 4.5 s.
- **Left as they are:**
  - the friend's alt text is "your friend" whatever the mood;
  - in *friend fell*, the friend stays tipped over after "help them up";
  - an actor's picture is cast, not typed by the kind of actor;
  - on a phone in landscape (844×390), the choices run below the screen (the phone pass is M4's);
  - a long name for Buddy could push the birthday scene's question past 4.5 s.
- **For the parent** (in Task 26's playtest list, below):
  - the friend sobbing in two scenes;
  - Buddy's small smile while a feelings scene plays;
  - the kindness choices' names, which are written but never said.

**Task 21 — the game screen and the celebration**
- **"Again?" came after the answer.** Buddy said `fb.your_turn_again` ("Again?" or "One more time?") only once the child had tapped the Again button. When the button appeared he said nothing, so a child who can't read saw only an icon. Now he asks as the button appears, once the cheer has been heard. A tap says nothing more and starts the turn again.
- **A double tap skipped "your turn".**
  - The first tap ended the dance. The second landed while Buddy showed the move, and ended the celebration.
  - Task 23's App still counted the set as having had its turn, so the next one was two or three sets away.
  - Now a tap while Buddy shows the move does nothing. Tapping Buddy skips from "Your turn!" onwards.
- **The sticker flies into the book.** Before, it ended as a faint card in an empty corner. Now:
  - The celebration draws the sticker book where the Map's is, and the sticker flies into it and is gone (spec §4.5).
  - Its picture scales with the screen's height (139 px on a tablet). On a phone in landscape it no longer covers Buddy's head and runs off the top.
- **Buddy during the bridge line.** When counting bridged back to give-to-Buddy, the corner Buddy was hidden while he said the bridge line, though the round that puts him on stage hadn't begun. He now stays in the corner until it does.
- **Tests.** There are two more, 14 in the folder: the double tap, and the corner Buddy during the bridge.
  - "Your turn" now checks the offer: said once, after the cheer.
  - The 🏠 test played no round and kept the screen mounted, so it couldn't see what it names. It now plays every round of a set, taps 🏠 just after the last one ends, and unmounts as the App does. It checks that the rounds stay recorded, with no result, no sticker and no celebration.
- **Checked and fine.** The review checked every topic's first round in the browser pane, at 1180×820 and 1080×810:
  - The bar is 104 px tall, 🏠 and 🔊 are 72 px, and the corner Buddy covers no target.
  - Leaving at any point (during the bridge, between rounds, just after the last round) says nothing more and writes no result.
  - The music stops on every way out.
  - The pause after a reaction fits every case: a kind answer after an unkind miss, an answer Buddy showed, and the last round.
- **Left as they are:**
  - A mechanic that ended a round twice would record it twice. `useRoundFlow` ends a round once, and `GameScreen` trusts it.
  - `GameScreen` relies on the App to unmount it on 🏠.
  - The boundary doesn't catch an exception in a mechanic's event handler or timer. 🏠 still works there, and the quiet spell keeps prompting. A window `error` listener would also catch unrelated errors and pull the child out of a working set.
  - The celebration's lines have no caption, and Buddy's size is read once from the window's height.
  - Spec §4.5's "your turn" comes "with music", but the music stops after the dance.
- **For the parent** (in Task 26's tablet list, below): the numbers 1, 2 and 3 appear on a timer, not with the voice. Do they appear as Buddy says them?

**Task 22 — the Map, Hello, setup, the sticker book**
- **Return in setup started the game.** The form has a submit button, so the iPad keyboard's Return submitted it as soon as the child's name was typed.
  - In the browser, typing "Asha" and pressing Return went straight to Hello. The profile was saved grey, with a cap and no Buddy name.
  - 1B can't edit or delete a profile, so doing it again leaves a second Asha on Hello.
  - Return now closes the keyboard in both fields (`enterKeyHint="done"`), and only "Let's play!" starts.
- **The sticker count swallowed taps.** The yellow number on the Map's sticker book sits on top of the button, and it is what a child taps. It now lets taps through (`pointer-events: none`).
- **Smaller changes.**
  - The pointing hand sat above the suggested spot and covered the island's picture or the end of its title. It now points up from below the spot.
  - Hello's + is hidden while Buddy greets. Before, a tap on it in those 2.8 s opened Setup, and then the Map swept it away.
  - Stickers and empty places in the book are the same size (`box-sizing`).
  - The name suggestions say which one is chosen (`aria-pressed`).
- **Tests.** There is one more (21 in the folder), plus new checks in the existing tests:
  - Return doesn't submit setup;
  - while Buddy greets, Hello starts once and hides the +;
  - a topic with no game the app can play gets no spot;
  - the Map scrolls to the suggested spot.
- **Checked and fine.** The review ran the App in the browser pane at 1180×820 and 1080×810:
  - The suggestion is said once per arrival, and the suggested spot was in full view in all 8 arrivals, even at the far right.
  - A failed suggestion leaves the Map usable.
  - The sticker book opens on the latest sticker's page, and a long page scrolls.
  - On the first run, a suggested name is spoken when tapped, before Hello's tap.
  - A whitespace-only name can't start.
  - Every target the child uses is at least 64 px, and every Buddy, swatch, tab and spot is labelled.
- **Left as they are:**
  - Hello's + is 58 px at 0.6 opacity: fine for a grown-up, and Plan 1C's gate will cover it.
  - The OpenMoji credit is below the fold on a tablet, though "Let's play!" is in view.
  - The Map's suggestion and its spots count playable topics differently (they agree in M1).
  - A page with 12 or more stickers shows no empty place.
  - Hello and Setup read the window's height once.
  - The suggested spot's bounce overrides its press-down.

**Task 23 — the app**
- **Step 5 ("Play it")** was the controller's, in the desktop app's browser pane at 1180×820, with the dev server. It covered:
  - setup (Return stayed on the form) → Hello → the greeting → the Map with its suggestion;
  - a whole Letters set, through its celebration and back to the Map, with the sticker count at 1;
  - the sticker book;
  - a Counting set left with 🏠 (the count stayed at 1);
  - a real drag-to-match round.

  It found the next two items.
- **The sticker count still didn't open the book.** Task 22 let taps through the number, but the button is round, and the middle of the number lies outside its circle, so a tap there hit nothing. The number now opens the book itself.
- **Buddy's size** was read from the window once. Setup's Buddy drew at 0 px when the page loaded before the window had a size. Hello, Setup and the celebration now follow the window (`useViewport`).
- **Saving a child twice.** A second tap on "Let's play!" while the first saved added the child twice, and 1B can't delete one. A second tap now does nothing until the save ends. The save is capped by `within()`.
- **When storage fails at start-up.**
  - If reading the profiles gave no answer, the blank boot screen stayed for good. If it failed, Setup opened, though nothing could be saved there.
  - Both now show a new `Trouble` screen: "Buddy could not start. Tap to try again", with the again icon, which reloads. Reloading is what cures Safari's stalled database.
  - A failed save, a visit that can't open, and `createServices` failing show it too. Before, a failed visit left the child on Hello with every Buddy disabled.
  - `AppBoundary` around the app shows it after a render error anywhere, too. Before, the page went blank, and an app on the home screen has no reload.
- **The greeting was cut off.** In Chrome, "Hi Asha! What a lovely evening!" ends at about 3.2 s, after the Map's 2.8 s, and the Map's suggestion cut its last word. The Map now waits for the greeting to end. It still opens no sooner than 2.8 s and no later than `GREETING_MAX_MS` (5 s).
- **Keeping the data** (§10). The browser is now asked at every start that finds profiles, not only when a child is added. That is usually before the app is installed, and Android Chrome then says no, with nothing asking again.
- **The picture guard** compares names exactly, against a listing of `public/art`. macOS found `ui/Home.svg` for `ui/home.svg`, and GitHub Pages would not.
- **Tests.** The App tests went from 5 to 10 (11 with the picture guard):
  - the first run asks to keep the data, and the audio is unlocked inside the tap;
  - a double tap saves once;
  - a stall at start-up shows "Try again";
  - a visit whose reads stall or fail still reaches the Map, with the failures counted;
  - the Map waits for the greeting;
  - the boundary;
  - the long test also counts the "your turn"s: 3 to 5 in its 10 sets.

  `screens.test.tsx` checks that Buddy follows the window. `map.test.tsx` checks the sticker count opens the book.
- **Left as they are:**
  - A second child can play only after a reload: Plan 1C's wind-down leads back to Hello.
  - An app left open overnight carries on yesterday's visit (a Task 26 check, and a Plan 1C note).
  - After a storage failure, the visit records its rounds under session 1.
  - The record clock uses `Date.now`.
  - The re-read of the stickers after a set isn't capped.
  - Once in the Chromium pane, the first greeting right after a reload stayed silent until the speech timeout. It couldn't be repeated, and Task 26's Hello item asks for it on the Android tablet.

**Task 24 — installable and offline**
- **Step 4 ("Serve the build and look")** was the controller's, in the desktop app's browser pane, with `npx vite preview --base /buddy/`.
  - The service worker was active under `/buddy/`, all 157 precache entries were cached, Andika loaded, and no picture was broken.
  - With the server stopped, a reload still opened the app. Setup → Hello → the Map worked offline, with every picture.
- **Set up after adding Buddy to the home screen.** On an iPad, the home-screen app keeps its own storage, apart from Safari's. A parent who set the child up in Safari and then added Buddy to the home screen would open an empty Buddy. The child stayed behind in Safari.
  - Setup's tip (Task 22's file) now says to add Buddy to the home screen first and set up there.
  - Task 25's README and Task 26's checklist say so too (below).
- **The iOS icon is square.** iOS rounds the corners itself and shows transparent pixels as black, so `apple-touch-icon.png` is rendered without the rounded corners. The other three icons are unchanged.
- **`dev:tablet`'s comment** said the tablet needs https for the service worker as well. Vite's dev server registers none, and a self-signed certificate would block one, so install and offline need the published build. The task's text above makes the same claim.
- **Checked and fine:**
  - No page is reloaded under a playing child: the app doesn't import `virtual:pwa-register`, and the build is a single chunk, so a worker that takes over mid-set can't strand a file. The child sees a new version on the cold start after it downloaded.
  - `navigateFallback` and `start_url` resolve under `/buddy/`.
  - With every voice clip (a dry run plans 265), the app stays near 3.5 MB, inside §11's 5 MB.
  - Buddy sits well inside the maskable icon's safe circle.
  - Re-rendering gives identical PNGs.
- **Left as they are:**
  - The three manifest icons are precached twice (harmless; "157 entries" counts them).
  - Nothing new is unit-tested; the build output and the checklist cover it.
- **For before the voice clips ship** (in the notes for Plan 1C): Safari may not play precached clips. It asks for a byte range, and Workbox's precache answers with the whole file.

**Task 25 — publishing on GitHub Pages**
- **Keeping the child's details private.** The README's first publishing step read as if deleting `docs/` and pushing would do. That leaves the documents in every earlier commit. The step now says:
  - every commit shows the parent's git email;
  - a Pages site is public even from a private repository (the child's data stays on the tablet);
  - a public repository needs a new history that never held `docs/`;
  - publishing is when to decide whether to name a licensor for the drawn pictures, which the notes on Task 6 left to this step.
- **The workflow.**
  - It no longer cancels a deployment under way (`cancel-in-progress: false`, as in GitHub's Pages starters).
  - It takes the base path from `actions/configure-pages`, which now runs before the build. A project site builds under `/<repository>/` as before, and a `<user>.github.io` repository or a custom domain under `/`. Both builds were checked.
- **The README** also:
  - warns that the first run fails until Pages is switched on;
  - says that an online speech voice (some of Chrome's) sends the spoken lines to its provider, so "nothing leaves the device" holds on the iPad.
- **Checked and fine.**
  - Every action's major tag exists and is the latest, as of 2026-10-07: checkout and setup-node v7, configure-pages v6, upload-pages-artifact and deploy-pages v5.
  - Their inputs and permissions match the current documentation.
  - On a clone of the tracked files, with `CI=true TZ=UTC`, `npm ci`'s lockfile, the check and the build all pass.
  - All 232 picture names match the files' exact case, which a Linux server needs.
- **Left as they are:**
  - permissions per job (a small gain);
  - no check on pull requests (one person's repository).
- **For the parent** (in the notes for Plan 1C): the school's initials reach the built app in two templates' `examFormat` labels. Nothing reads them, but they should be renamed if the site or repository is public.
